    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

exp_id: exp003\\n    role: primary\\n    result: failed - bootstrap failure, 95% invalid actions, pg_loss≈0\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D002\\n- id: D004\\n  date: 2026-05-14 00:10 UTC\\n  summary: 采用方案 A（修改解析器适配 Qwen3 格式）+ prompt 增强 + 速度优化，解决 bootstrap 失败\\n  rationale: 根因确认：Qwen3-1.7B 原生输出 &lt;think>...&lt;/think> 后直接跟自然语言，不生成 &lt;answer> 标签。RAGEN\\n    解析器正则要求 &lt;answer> 导致 95% invalid。方案 A 最快（1-2h 改代码）、最低风险（不换模型不加训练阶段），同时优化速度（关闭 collapse\\n    multi_turn 省 35% 步耗时 + 减 traj/step）。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: exp003\\n    role: diagnostic\\n    result: '32/32 samples confirm: no &lt;answer> tag in Qwen3 output'\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D003\\n- id: D005\\n  date: 2026-05-14 02:56 UTC\\n  summary: exp007 borderline 但不 pivot SFT，提高 LR 到 5e-6 重跑 GRPO\\n  rationale: exp007 step 10 action_is_valid=8.35%，技术上触发预设 kill 条件（<10%），但：(1) 趋势明确上升\\n    4.01%→8.35%（+0.48pp/step），线性外推 ~step 14 即突破 10%；(2) success 从 0%→6.25%，GRPO 确实在学习；(3)\\n    manager_invalid 6.46%→1.56%，解析器修复有效；(4) 崩溃是 /tmp 磁盘满（基础设施），非算法失败；(5) SFT warmup\\n    需额外收集 expert trajectories + 训练周期，开销大。因此override kill 条件，选择提高 LR（1e-6→5e-6）重跑 GRPO\\n    30 步，save_freq=5，同时修复所有基础设施问题（RAY_TMPDIR、/tmp 清理）。新 kill 条件：step 10 action_is_valid\\n    < 15%（因 LR 提高 5x，预期收敛更快）。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: exp007\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: step 10 action_is_valid=8.35%, success=6.25%, 因磁盘满崩溃于 step 11, checkpoint\\n      全部丢失\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D004\\n- id: D006\\n  date: 2026-05-14 03:02 UTC\\n  summary: 撤回 exp008 (LR 5e-6)，改用 SFT warmup 再接 GRPO\\n  rationale: Agent 正确指出 action_is_valid 低的根因不是 LR 不够高，而是 Qwen3-1.7B 缺乏 ALFWorld 命令语义知识——92%\\n    的动作语法正确但语义无效（不是合法 ALFWorld 命令）。提高 LR 只能加速已有信号的收敛，无法从根本解决命令词汇缺失。SFT warmup 直接教模型\\n    ALFWorld 命令格式和语义，GRPO 再优化策略，是更高效的路径。SFT 计划已就绪（artifacts/sft_warmup_plan.md），总成本\\n    ~8h（SFT 2h + GRPO 6h），可接受。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: exp007\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: 10 步 action_is_valid 仅 8.35%，语法正确但语义无效，指向命令知识缺失而非学习速度问题\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D005\\n- id: D007\\n  date: 2026-05-14 05:40 UTC\\n  summary: Kill exp011，改 reward function（连续score+降format penalty+group_size 16），从\\n    step 5 checkpoint 恢复\\n  rationale: Reward 分析确认：format_penalty(-0.1/turn)在零成功 group 中劫持 GRPO 梯度，模型只学格式不学任务（action_is_valid\\n    68% + success 0%）。A1 连续 score 给部分完成的轨迹提供梯度信号；A2 降 format_penalty 到 -0.01 避免淹没任务信号；B1\\n    group_size 8→16 提高正信号 group 比例（32%→53%）。从 step 5 恢复因其仍有 1.6% success 残余。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: exp010\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: failed - reward analysis revealed format penalty hijacking GRPO gradient\\n  - exp_id: exp011\\n    role: killed\\n    result: same reward config as exp010, will repeat collapse pattern\\n  depends_on:\\n  - Mode mapping: RAGEN mode -> AlfWorld train_eval\\n    MODE_MAP = {\\n        'train': 'train',\\n        'val': None,  # Will use config.eval_dataset\\n        'test': None,  # Will use config.eval_dataset\\n    }\\n\\n    def __init__(self, config: AlfredEnvConfig = AlfredEnvConfig(), mode='train'):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Initialize AlfWorld environment.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            config: AlfredEnvConfig instance\\n            mode: 'train' or 'val' - determines which dataset split to use\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        super().__init__()\\n        self.config = config\\n        self.raw_env_config = load_config(self.config.config_file)\\n\\n        # Map RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval\\n        self.current_mode = mode\\n        self._alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        # Initialize raw environment\\n        self.raw_env = self._get_cached_raw_env(self._alfworld_mode)\\n        self.num_games = self.raw_env.num_games\\n        self.game_files = list(self.raw_env.game_files)\\n\\n        self.alfred_env = None\\n        self.current_game_file = None\\n        self.render_cache = None\\n        self.available_actions = None\\n        self.render_mode = self.config.render_mode\\n        assert self.render_mode == 'text'\\n\\n    def _get_alfworld_mode(self, mode: str) -> str:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Convert RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval string.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if mode == 'train':\\n            return 'train'\\n        else:\\n            # Use eval_dataset from config for val/test modes\\n            return self.config.eval_dataset\\n\\n    def _get_cached_raw_env(self, alfworld_mode: str) -> AlfredTWEnv:\\n        cache_key = (os.path.abspath(self.config.config_file), alfworld_mode)\\n        raw_env = _RAW_ENV_CACHE.get(cache_key)\\n        if raw_env is None:\\n            raw_env = AlfredTWEnv(config=self.raw_env_config, train_eval=alfworld_mode)\\n            _RAW_ENV_CACHE[cache_key] = raw_env\\n        return raw_env\\n    \\n    def _reinitialize_for_mode(self, mode: str):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Reinitialize the environment when mode changes.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        new_alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        if new_alfworld_mode != self._alfworld_mode:\\n            # Close existing environment\\n            if hasattr(self, 'alfred_env') and self.alfred_env is not None:\\n                try:\\n                    self.alfred_env.close()\\n                except Exception:\\n                    pass\\n\\n            # Reinitialize with new mode\\n            self._alfworld_mode = new_alfworld_mode\\n            self.current_mode = mode\\n            self.raw_env = self._get_cached_raw_env(self._alfworld_mode)\\n            self.num_games = self.raw_env.num_games\\n            self.game_files = list(self.raw_env.game_files)\\n            self.alfred_env = None\\n\\n    def reset(self, seed=None, mode=None):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Reset the environment with a specific seed.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            seed: Random seed for game selection\\n            mode: 'train', 'val', or 'test' - if different from current mode,\\n                  reinitializes the environment with the new dataset split\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            observation: Initial observation text with admissible actions\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        try:\\n            # Handle mode switching dynamically\\n            if mode is not None and mode != self.current_mode:\\n                self._reinitialize_for_mode(mode)\\n            elif mode is not None:\\n\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 1086 字符] ...\\n\\nx_steps = self.raw_env_config[\\\"rl\\\"][\\\"training\\\"][\\\"max_nb_steps_per_episode\\\"]\\n\\n            env_id = textworld.gym.register_game(\\n                selected_game,\\n                request_infos=request_infos,\\n                batch_size=1,\\n                asynchronous=False,\\n                max_episode_steps=max_steps,\\n                wrappers=wrappers\\n            )\\n\\n            self.alfred_env = textworld.gym.make(env_id)\\n\\n            obs, info = self.alfred_env.reset()\\n            self.available_actions = info[\\\"admissible_commands\\\"][0]\\n            self.instruction_text = obs[0]\\n            # Include admissible actions in render cache\\n            self.render_cache = self._format_observation(obs[0], self.available_actions)\\n            return self.render_cache\\n\\n        except (RuntimeError, RuntimeWarning) as e:\\n            print(f\\\"Error in reset: {e}\\\")\\n            next_seed = abs(hash(str(seed))) % (2 ** 32) if seed is not None else None\\n            return self.reset(next_seed, mode=self.current_mode)\\n\\n    def _format_observation(self, obs: str, admissible_actions: list) -> str:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Format observation to include admissible actions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        actions_str = \\\", \\\".join(admissible_actions)\\n        return f\\\"{obs}\\\\n\\\\nAdmissible actions: [{actions_str}]\\\"\\n    \\n    def step(self, action: str):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Take a step in the environment using the provided action string.\\n\\n        Reward scheme (matching verl-agent):\\n        - reward = config.score * won (10.0 on success, 0.0 otherwise)\\n\\n        Args:\\n            action: The action string to execute\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            observation: Updated observation with admissible actions\\n            reward: Sparse reward (score on success, 0 otherwise)\\n            done: Whether episode is finished\\n            info: Additional information dict\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        action_is_available = action in self.available_actions\\n\\n        # Execute action in environment\\n        obs, _, dones, infos = self.alfred_env.step([action])\\n        observation = obs[0]\\n        self.available_actions = infos[\\\"admissible_commands\\\"][0]\\n        done = dones[0]\\n        won = infos[\\\"won\\\"][0]\\n\\n        # Simplified reward matching verl-agent: score * won\\n        reward = self.config.score * float(won)\\n\\n        # Format observation with admissible actions\\n        self.render_cache = self._format_observation(observation, self.available_actions)\\n\\n        info = {\\n            \\\"action_is_effective\\\": True,\\n            \\\"action_is_valid\\\": action_is_available,\\n            \\\"success\\\": won\\n        }\\n\\n        return self.render_cache, reward, done, info\\n    \\n    def render(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Return current observation with admissible actions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        return self.render_cache\\n\\n    def close(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Clean up environment resources.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self.render_cache = None\\n        if hasattr(self, 'alfred_env') and self.alfred_env is not None:\\n            try:\\n                self.alfred_env.close()\\n            except Exception:\\n                pass\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    import os\\n    os.environ[\\\"ALFWORLD_DATA\\\"] = os.path.expanduser(\\\"~/.cache/alfworld\\\")\\n\\n    # Test basic environment functionality\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n=== Test 1: Basic environment with train mode ===\\\")\\n    env = AlfredTXTEnv()\\n    obs = env.reset(seed=42, mode='train')\\n    print(f\\\"Train observation (first 500 chars):\\\\n{obs[:500]}...\\\")\\n\\n    # Test mode switching\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n=== Test 2: Mode switching to val ===\\\")\\n    obs = env.reset(seed=42, mode='val')\\n    print(f\\\"Val observation (first 500 chars):\\\\n{obs[:500]}...\\\")\\n\\n    # Test step with admissible action\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n=== Test 3: Taking a step ===\\\")\\n    if env.available_actions:\\n        action = env.available_actions[0]\\n        print(f\\\"Taking action: {action}\\\")\\n        obs, reward, done, info = env.step(action)\\n        print(f\\\"Reward: {reward}, Done: {done}, Info: {info}\\\")\\n        print(f\\\"New observation (first 300 chars):\\\\n{obs[:300]}...\\\")\\n\\n    env.close()\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n=== All tests completed ===\\\")\\n    \\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.95}}"}

Reinitialize with new mode\\n            self._alfworld_mode = new_alfworld_mode\\n            self.current_mode = mode\\n            self.raw_env = self._get_cached_raw_env(self._alfworld_mode)\\n            self.num_games = self.raw_env.num_games\\n            self.game_files = list(self.raw_env.game_files)\\n            self.alfred_env = None\\n\\n    def reset(self, seed=None, mode=None):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Reset the environment with a specific seed.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            seed: Random seed for game selection\\n            mode: 'train', 'val', or 'test' - if different from current mode,\\n                  reinitializes the environment with the new dataset split\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            observation: Initial observation text with admissible actions\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        try:\\n            # Handle mode switching dynamically\\n            if mode is not None and mode != self.current_mode:\\n                self._reinitialize_for_mode(mode)\\n            elif mode is not None:\\n\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 1086 字符] ...\\n\\nx_steps = self.raw_env_config[\\\"rl\\\"][\\\"training\\\"][\\\"max_nb_steps_per_episode\\\"]\\n\\n            env_id = textworld.gym.register_game(\\n                selected_game,\\n                request_infos=request_infos,\\n                batch_size=1,\\n                asynchronous=False,\\n                max_episode_steps=max_steps,\\n                wrappers=wrappers\\n            )\\n\\n            self.alfred_env = textworld.gym.make(env_id)\\n\\n            obs, info = self.alfred_env.reset()\\n            self.available_actions = info[\\\"admissible_commands\\\"][0]\\n            self.instruction_text = obs[0]\\n            # Include admissible actions in render cache\\n            self.render_cache = self._format_observation(obs[0], self.available_actions)\\n            return self.render_cache\\n\\n        except (RuntimeError, RuntimeWarning) as e:\\n            print(f\\\"Error in reset: {e}\\\")\\n            next_seed = abs(hash(str(seed))) % (2 ** 32) if seed is not None else None\\n            return self.reset(next_seed, mode=self.current_mode)\\n\\n    def _format_observation(self, obs: str, admissible_actions: list) -> str:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Format observation to include admissible actions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        actions_str = \\\", \\\".join(admissible_actions)\\n        return f\\\"{obs}\\\\n\\\\nAdmissible actions: [{actions_str}]\\\"\\n    \\n    def step(self, action: str):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Take a step in the environment using the provided action string.\\n\\n        Reward scheme (matching verl-agent):\\n        - reward = config.score * won (10.0 on success, 0.0 otherwise)\\n\\n        Args:\\n            action: The action string to execute\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            observation: Updated observation with admissible actions\\n            reward: Sparse reward (score on success, 0 otherwise)\\n            done: Whether episode is finished\\n            info: Additional information dict\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        action_is_available = action in self.available_actions\\n\\n        # Execute action in environment\\n        obs, _, dones, infos = self.alfred_env.step([action])\\n        observation = obs[0]\\n        self.available_actions = infos[\\\"admissible_commands\\\"][0]\\n        done = dones[0]\\n        won = infos[\\\"won\\\"][0]\\n\\n        # Simplified reward matching verl-agent: score * won\\n        reward = self.config.score * float(won)\\n\\n        # Format observation with admissible actions\\n        self.render_cache = self._format_observation(observation, self.available_actions)\\n\\n        info = {\\n            \\\"action_is_effective\\\": True,\\n            \\\"action_is_valid\\\": action_is_available,\\n            \\\"success\\\": won\\n        }\\n\\n        return self.render_cache, reward, done, info\\n    \\n    def render(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Return current observation with admissible actions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        return self.render_cache\\n\\n    def close(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Clean up environment resources.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self.render_cache = None\\n        if hasattr(self, 'alfred_env') and self.alfred_env is not None:\\n            try:\\n                self.alfred_env.close()\\n            except Exception:\\n                pass\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    import os\\n    os.environ[\\\"ALFWORLD_DATA\\\"] = os.path.expanduser(\\\"~/.cache/alfworld\\\")\\n\\n    # Test mode switching\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n=== Test 2: Mode switching to val ===\\\")\\n    obs = env.reset(seed=42, mode='val')\\n    print(f\\\"Val observation (first 500 chars):\\\\n{obs[:500]}...\\\")\\n\\n    # Test step with admissible action\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n=== Test 3: Taking a step ===\\\")\\n    if env.available_actions:\\n        action = env.available_actions[0]\\n        print(f\\\"Taking action: {action}\\\")\\n        obs, reward, done, info = env.step(action)\\n        print(f\\\"Reward: {reward}, Done: {done}, Info: {info}\\\")\\n        print(f\\\"New observation (first 300 chars):\\\\n{obs[:300]}...\\\")\\n\\n    env.close()\\n    print(\\\"\\\\n=== All tests completed ===\\\")\\n    \\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.95}}"

Looking at the current env.py code:
1. The reward scheme is: `reward = self.config.score * float(won)` - this is purely binary (won=True/False), so `self.config.score` is just a scaling factor (probably 10.0), and the reward is either 10.0 or 0.0.


2. I need to check how `request_infos` is being set up and whether it's actually requesting the score information from AlfWorld.

The worker was named `worker_code_verify-alfworld-score`, which suggests they were investigating this exact scoring mechanism. Let me look at what changes were made in the next experiment to see if this was addressed.

_source line 51 · 2026-05-14T06:06:06.202Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Key findings so far:

1. **env.py line 137**: `request_infos = textworld.EnvInfos(won=True, admissible_commands=True, extras=["gamefile"])` — **no `score=True`**. This confirms D008's observation.

2. **Reward in env.py**: `reward = self.config.score * float(won)` — purely binary. `self.config.score` is a config constant (likely 10.0), `won` is boolean. So reward is either 0.0 or 10.0.

3. **format_penalty**: Only found in `es_manager.py:236`. 

The format penalty gets applied as part of the GRPO reward shaping. Now I'm trying to figure out what the worker `worker_code_verify-alfworld-score` was actually checking — likely whether `infos["score"]` is even available in ALFWorld (it's not, since it's not in request_infos), what granularity the score provides across different task types, and how the overall reward structure works. D008 flagged that A1 (progress reward) needs to first verify the granularity of `infos['score']`, which is the core problem the proposed A1 fix is trying to address.

Now I need to look at es_manager.py to understand the format_penalty logic and the full reward computation pipeline, plus check what artifacts the worker may have generated.
```

what seed is used to reset this environment\\n\\n\\n\\nclass EnvStateManager:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Manager for the environment state\\n    The class is responsible for managing multiple (kinds of) environments\\n    \\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    def __init__(self, config, mode: str = \\\"train\\\"):\\n        self.sys_config = config\\n        self.mode = mode\\n        self.config = getattr(self.sys_config.es_manager, mode)\\n        self.env_groups = int(self.config.env_groups)\\n        self.group_size = self.config.group_size\\n        seed_cfg = getattr(self.sys_config, \\\"seed\\\", None)\\n        if seed_cfg is not None:\\n            self.base_seed = seed_cfg.get(mode, None)\\n        else:\\n            self.base_seed = None\\n        self.seed_counter = 0\\n        self._init_envs()\\n        self.rollout_cache = None\\n        self._executors: Dict[str, ThreadPoolExecutor] = {}\\n        self._executors_shutdown = False\\n        self._register_parallel_executors()\\n        atexit.register(self._shutdown_executors)\\n\\n    def _init_envs(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Initialize the environments. train_envs and val_envs are lists of envs:\\n        Input: tags: [\\\"SimpleSokoban\\\", \\\"HarderSokoban\\\"]; n_groups: [1, 1]; group_size: 16\\n        Output: envs: List[Dict], each **entry** is a dict with keys: tag, group_id, env_id, env, env_config, status\\n        Example: [{\\\"tag\\\": \\\"SimpleSokoban\\\", \\\"group_id\\\": 0, \\\"env_id\\\": 0, \\\"env\\\": env, \\\"config\\\": env_config, \\\"status\\\": EnvStatus()},\\n            ...\\n            {\\\"tag\\\": \\\"SimpleSokoban\\\", \\\"group_id\\\": 0, \\\"env_id\\\": 15 (group_size - 1), ...},\\n            {\\\"tag\\\": \\\"HarderSokoban\\\", \\\"group_id\\\": 1, \\\"env_id\\\": 16, ...}\\n            ...]\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        assert sum(self.config.env_configs.n_groups) == self.env_groups, f\\\"Sum of n_groups must equal env_groups. Got {len(self.config.env_configs.tags)} != {len(self.config.env_configs.n_groups)}\\\"\\n        self.envs = self._init_env_instances(self.config)\\n\\n    def _init_env_instances(self, config):\\n        env_list = []\\n        done_groups = 0\\n        for tag, n_group in zip(config.env_configs.tags, config.env_configs.n_groups):\\n            for env_id in range(done_groups * self.group_size, (done_groups + n_group) * self.group_size):\\n                cfg_template = self.sys_config.custom_envs[tag]\\n                env_class = cfg_template.env_type\\n                max_actions_per_traj = cfg_template.max_actions_per_traj\\n                if cfg_template.env_config is None:\\n                    env_config = REGISTERED_ENV_CONFIGS[env_class]()\\n                else:\\n                    env_config = REGISTERED_ENV_CONFIGS[env_class](**cfg_template.env_config)\\n                env_obj = REGISTERED_ENVS[env_class](env_config)\\n                parallel_friendly = bool(getattr(cfg_template, \\\"parallel_friendly\\\", False))\\n                max_workers = int(getattr(cfg_template, \\\"max_workers\\\", 1) or 1)\\n                entry = {'tag': tag, 'group_id': env_id // self.group_size, 'env_id': env_id, \\n                        'env': env_obj, 'config': env_config, 'status': EnvStatus(), 'max\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 4754 字符] ...\\n\\nt[PIL.Image.Image]}]}\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        def _execute_actions(env, actions):\\n            acc_reward, turn_info, turn_done = 0, {}, False\\n            raw_acc_reward = 0.0\\n            executed_actions = []\\n            for action in actions:\\n                _, reward, done, info = env.step(action)\\n                acc_reward += reward\\n                try:\\n                    raw_acc_reward += float(info.get('raw_reward', 0.0))\\n                except Exception:\\n                    pass\\n                turn_info.update(info) # NOTE: currently use last info for multi-action\\n                executed_actions.append(action)\\n                if done:\\n                    turn_done = True\\n                    break\\n            try:\\n                turn_info['raw_reward'] = float(raw_acc_reward)\\n            except Exception:\\n                pass\\n            return acc_reward, turn_info, turn_done, executed_actions\\n\\n        def _log_env_state(status, history, cur_obs, max_actions_per_traj, executed_actions, all_actions, acc_reward, turn_done, turn_info, env_input):\\n            obs = self._handle_mm_state(cur_obs)\\n            status.num_actions += len(executed_actions)\\n            status.rewards.append(acc_reward) # NOTE use turn-wise acc_reward\\n            actions_left = max_actions_per_traj - status.num_actions\\n            if turn_done:\\n                status.terminated = True # TODO check terminated definition in gymnasium\\n                status.truncated = not turn_info.get('success', False)\\n            history = self._update_cache_history(history, next_state=obs, actions_left=actions_left, num_actions_info={\\n                'actions': executed_actions, 'reward': acc_reward, 'info': turn_info,\\n                'llm_response': env_input['llm_response'], 'llm_raw_response': env_input['llm_raw_response']\\n            })\\n            # filter out invalid actions\\n            # history = [content for content in history[:-1] if content['actions']] + [history[-1]]\\n            return status, history\\n\\n        envs = self.envs\\n        env_outputs = []\\n\\n        def _process_env_input(env_input: Dict) -> Dict:\\n            acc_reward, turn_info, turn_done = 0, {}, False\\n            entry = envs[env_input['env_id']]\\n            env_id, env = entry['env_id'], entry['env']\\n            actions_left_before = entry['max_actions_per_traj'] - entry['status'].num_actions\\n\\n            # execute actions in envs\\n            valid_actions = self._extract_map_valid_actions(entry, env_input['actions'])\\n            acc_reward, turn_info, turn_done, executed_actions = _execute_actions(env, valid_actions[:actions_left_before])\\n            no_manager_action = len(valid_actions) == 0\\n            penalty_delta = 0.0\\n            if len(valid_actions) != len(env_input['actions']) or not valid_actions:\\n                penalty_delta = self.sys_config.es_manager.format_penalty\\n            if no_manager_action:\\n                turn_info = dict(turn_info)\\n                turn_info['manager_invalid_action'] = True\\n\\n            status, history = _log_env_state(entry['status'], self.rollout_cache[env_id]['history'], entry['env'].render(), entry['max_actions_per_traj'], executed_actions, valid_actions, acc_reward, turn_done, turn_info, env_input)\\n            if no_manager_action and history:\\n                history[-1]['manager_invalid_action'] = True\\n            if status.num_actions >= entry['max_actions_per_traj'] and not turn_done:\\n                status.truncated = True\\n                status.terminated = True\\n                turn_done = True\\n\\n            return {\\n                'env_id': env_id,\\n                'status': status,\\n                'history': history,\\n                'turn_done': turn_done,\\n                'penalty_delta': penalty_delta,\\n            }\\n\\n        results: List[Optional[Dict]] = [None] * len(all_env_inputs)\\n        tag2items: Dict[str, List[tuple]] = {}\\n        for idx, env_input in enumerate(all_env_inputs):\\n            entry = envs[env_input['env_id']]\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.41}}"}

Key findings so far:

1. **env.py line 137**: `request_infos = textworld.EnvInfos(won=True, admissible_commands=True, extras=["gamefile"])` — **NO `score=True`**. This means `infos["score"]` is NOT available. D008's concern about A1 (progress reward) needing score granularity verification is validated.


2. The reward function in env.py's step() is purely binary—it only rewards task completion, not intermediate progress. Meanwhile, es_manager.py applies a format penalty when the model's actions don't match valid commands, which could be skewing the gradient signal that GRPO relies on. No recent file modifications suggest the worker was investigating these mechanisms rather than making changes.

3. I need to check what format_penalty values were actually used in exp010 and exp011, and see if the worker left any findings about whether D008's proposal to use continuous progress-based rewards from `infos["score"]` would help counteract the format penalty's effect on learning.

Mode mapping: RAGEN mode -> AlfWorld train_eval\\n    MODE_MAP = {\\n        'train': 'train',\\n        'val': None,  # Will use config.eval_dataset\\n        'test': None,  # Will use config.eval_dataset\\n    }\\n\\n    def __init__(self, config: AlfredEnvConfig = AlfredEnvConfig(), mode='train'):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Initialize AlfWorld environment.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            config: AlfredEnvConfig instance\\n            mode: 'train' or 'val' - determines which dataset split to use\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        super().__init__()\\n        self.config = config\\n        self.raw_env_config = load_config(self.config.config_file)\\n\\n        # Map RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval\\n        self.current_mode = mode\\n        self._alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        # Initialize raw environment\\n        self.raw_env = self._get_cached_raw_env(self._alfworld_mode)\\n        self.num_games = self.raw_env.num_games\\n        self.game_files = list(self.raw_env.game_files)\\n\\n        self.alfred_env = None\\n        self.current_game_file = None\\n        self.render_cache = None\\n        self.available_actions = None\\n        self.render_mode = self.config.render_mode\\n        assert self.render_mode == 'text'\\n\\n    def _get_alfworld_mode(self, mode: str) -> str:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Convert RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval string.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if mode == 'train':\\n            return 'train'\\n        else:\\n            # Use eval_dataset from config for val/test modes\\n            return self.config.eval_dataset\\n\\n    def _get_cached_raw_env(self, alfworld_mode: str) -> AlfredTWEnv:\\n        cache_key = (os.path.abspath(self.config.config_file), alfworld_mode)\\n        raw_env = _RAW_ENV_CACHE.get(cache_key)\\n        if raw_env is None:\\n            raw_env = AlfredTWEnv(config=self.raw_env_config, train_eval=alfworld_mode)\\n            _RAW_ENV_CACHE[cache_key] = raw_env\\n        return raw_env\\n    \\n    def _reinitialize_for_mode(self, mode: str):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Reinitialize the environment when mode changes.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        new_alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        if new_alfworld_mode != self._alfworld_mode:\\n            # Close existing environment\\n            if hasattr(self, 'alfred_env') and self.alfred_env is not None:\\n                try:\\n                    self.alfred_env.close()\\n                except Exception:\\n                    pass\\n\\n            # Reinitialize with new mode\\n            self._alfworld_mode = new_alfworld_mode\\n            self.current_mode = mode\\n            self.raw_env = self._get_cached_raw_env(self._alfworld_mode)\\n            self.num_games = self.raw_env.num_games\\n            self.game_files = list(self.raw_env.game_files)\\n            self.alfred_env = None\\n\\n    def reset(self, seed=None, mode=None):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Reset the environment with a specific seed.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            seed: Random seed for game selection\\n            mode: 'train', 'val', or 'test' - if different from current mode,\\n                  reinitializes the environment with the new dataset split\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            observation: Initial observation text with admissible actions\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        try:\\n            # Handle mode switching dynamically\\n            if mode is not None and mode != self.current_mode:\\n                self._reinitialize_for_mode(mode)\\n            elif mode is not None:\\n\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 190 字符] ...\\n\\nError(\\\"Seed must be provided in test mode.\\\")\\n                selected_game = self.game_files[seed % len(self.game_files)]\\n            else:\\n                if seed is not None:\\n                    np.random.seed(seed)\\n                    random.seed(seed)\\n                    game_idx = seed % len(self.game_files)\\n                    selected_game = self.game_files[game_idx]\\n                else:\\n                    selected_game = random.choice(self.game_files)\\n\\n            self.current_game_file = selected_game\\n\\n            if hasattr(self, 'alfred_env') and self.alfred_env is not None:\\n                try:\\n                    self.alfred_env.close()\\n                except Exception:\\n                    pass\\n\\n            request_infos = textworld.EnvInfos(won=True, admissible_commands=True, extras=[\\\"gamefile\\\"])\\n            wrappers = [AlfredDemangler(), AlfredInfos()]\\n            max_steps = self.raw_env_config[\\\"rl\\\"][\\\"training\\\"][\\\"max_nb_steps_per_episode\\\"]\\n\\n            env_id = textworld.gym.register_game(\\n                selected_game,\\n                request_infos=request_infos,\\n                batch_size=1,\\n                asynchronous=False,\\n                max_episode_steps=max_steps,\\n                wrappers=wrappers\\n            )\\n\\n            self.alfred_env = textworld.gym.make(env_id)\\n\\n            obs, info = self.alfred_env.reset()\\n            self.available_actions = info[\\\"admissible_commands\\\"][0]\\n            self.instruction_text = obs[0]\\n            # Include admissible actions in render cache\\n            self.render_cache = self._format_observation(obs[0], self.available_actions)\\n            return self.render_cache\\n\\n        except (RuntimeError, RuntimeWarning) as e:\\n            print(f\\\"Error in reset: {e}\\\")\\n            next_seed = abs(hash(str(seed))) % (2 ** 32) if seed is not None else None\\n            return self.reset(next_seed, mode=self.current_mode)\\n\\n    def _format_observation(self, obs: str, admissible_actions: list) -> str:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Format observation to include admissible actions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        actions_str = \\\", \\\".join(admissible_actions)\\n        return f\\\"{obs}\\\\n\\\\nAdmissible actions: [{actions_str}]\\\"\\n    \\n    def step(self, action: str):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Take a step in the environment using the provided action string.\\n\\n        Reward scheme (D007 fix):\\n        - won: config.score (10.0)\\n        - valid action: 0.1 (continuous proxy for GRPO gradient)\\n        - invalid action: 0.0\\n\\n        Args:\\n            action: The action string to execute\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            observation: Updated observation with admissible actions\\n            reward: Continuous reward (10.0 success, 0.1 valid action, 0.0 invalid)\\n            done: Whether episode is finished\\n            info: Additional information dict\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        action_is_available = action in self.available_actions\\n\\n        # Execute action in environment\\n        obs, _, dones, infos = self.alfred_env.step([action])\\n        observation = obs[0]\\n        self.available_actions = infos[\\\"admissible_commands\\\"][0]\\n        done = dones[0]\\n        won = infos[\\\"won\\\"][0]\\n\\n        # D007-A1: valid-action bonus creates GRPO gradient in all-failure groups\\n        # ALFWorld has no continuous score; use admissible-action signal as proxy\\n        if won:\\n            reward = self.config.score  # 10.0 on task completion\\n        elif action_is_available:\\n            reward = 0.1  # small reward for valid env interaction\\n        else:\\n            reward = 0.0\\n\\n        # Format observation with admissible actions\\n        self.render_cache = self._format_observation(observation, self.available_actions)\\n\\n        info = {\\n            \\\"action_is_effective\\\": True,\\n            \\\"action_is_valid\\\": action_is_available,\\n            \\\"success\\\": won,\\n            \\\"raw_reward\\\": reward,\\n        }\\n\\n        return self.render_cache, reward, done, info\\n    \\n    def render(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Return current observation with admissible actions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        return self.render_cache\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.34}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the full picture. The worker `worker_code_verify-alfworld-score` has done its work — and I can see the code changes:

**env.py has been modified with D007-A1 implementation**:
- OLD: `reward = self.config.score * float(won)` — pure binary (0 or 10.0)
- NEW: Three-tier reward:
  - `won → 10.0` (task completion)
  - `action_is_available → 0.1` (valid command)
  - `else → 0.0` (invalid action)


- Also added `"raw_reward": reward` to the info dict with a note that ALFWorld lacks continuous scoring, so the admissible-action signal serves as a proxy

**exp012 config also exists** with `format_penalty: -0.01` for D007-A2, `group_size: 16` bumped up from 8 for D007-B1, and it resumes from the `global_step_5` checkpoint.

Now I'm reviewing these changes critically as the Reviewer to check whether the A1 implementation is correct — the worker confirmed that ALFWorld indeed has no continuous score, validating D008's original concern, and implemented the fallback accordingly.

The valid-action bonus of 0.1 per action is worth scrutinizing though. With tasks running ~30 turns and multiple valid actions possible, a trajectory that stays valid throughout but never completes could rack up 3.0 reward, which is 30% of the success reward. That's a meaningful chunk and could push the model toward just producing valid actions rather than actually solving tasks.

The interaction with format_penalty looks reasonable — at -0.01, it's negligible compared to the 0.1 bonus, so the valid-action signal should dominate as intended.

Looking at how RAGEN actually accumulates these rewards, the system collects per-turn accumulated rewards and then sums them across the trajectory. With the exp012 config setting max_actions_per_turn to 1, each turn contributes exactly one action's worth of reward, so the math checks out at 30 × 0.1 = 3.0 maximum from valid actions alone.

The key insight is that GRPO's advantage estimation works on relative rewards within a batch—if trajectories are failing, the ones with more valid actions naturally get higher scores and positive advantages, which trains the model to produce valid actions as intended. However, there's a real risk the model could game this by repeatedly producing trivial but valid actions like "look" to rack up bonuses without actually progressing on the task. I should flag this as a potential issue.

Now I need to verify the checkpoint metrics from step 5 to see where the training actually stood before resuming.

The step 5 checkpoint directory exists with the actor weights intact. Both GPUs are currently idle since the worker only ran verification checks and hasn't launched training yet. Looking at what's been validated so far: ALFWorld confirmed it has no continuous score signal (which was a prerequisite concern), the valid-action bonus of 0.1 per turn is a reasonable proxy, and the exp012 config correctly implements the format penalty reduction and increased group size. The main remaining concerns are the reward hacking risk and whether the checkpoint metrics actually show meaningful progress before resuming. Tracing through the reward flow in the execution pipeline — the accumulated reward from `env.step()` gets combined with a format penalty if the action list is invalid or empty, and I need to verify how this penalty is actually applied downstream in the GRPO training loop.

Looking at the reward structure across different scenarios in exp012, I can see the gradient it creates: successfully completing the task yields 10.0, valid but unsuccessful actions accumulate 0.1 per turn, and format errors incur a -0.01 penalty. This means a task completion on turn 15 nets 11.4 total reward, while 30 turns of valid but unsuccessful actions only yields 3.0, establishing a clear hierarchy that should guide the model toward both task success and valid formatting.

There's a risk of the model defaulting to repetitive "look" commands since it's a safe valid action, but the KL loss constraint keeps it anchored to the SFT distribution, and the 10.0 success reward is strong enough to outweigh this tendency. I'll note this as a potential concern but not a blocker.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I understand the full reward pipeline:

1. **env.py step()**: Returns `reward` per action (0.0, 0.1, or 10.0 in exp012)
2. **es_manager.py**: 
   - Accumulates `acc_reward` per turn (sum of action rewards)
   - Accumulates `penalty` per trajectory (sum of format_penalty across turns with format errors)
3. **ctx_manager.py line 288-292**:
   - `normalized_acc_scores` = normalized accumulated scores (from env rewards)
   - Combined with penalty to produce the final trajectory reward for GRPO

So the trajectory reward is the normalized environment reward plus the accumulated penalty. Looking at exp012 specifically, a successful trajectory that wins at turn 15 gets 11.4 points with no penalty, while one that runs all 30 turns without winning scores 3.0, and an all-invalid trajectory gets heavily penalized at -0.3. Mixed trajectories fall somewhere in between depending on the ratio of valid to invalid turns.

The key insight here is that this reward structure actually solves the gradient problem from exp010 — by giving a small bonus for each valid action, trajectories within a failure group now have meaningful reward differences that GRPO can learn from, rather than all receiving identical rewards.

The worker verified that ALFWorld doesn't expose continuous scores in the info dict and modified the environment's step function to implement the reward calculation directly.

However, there's a potential reward hacking issue: the 0.1 per-valid-action bonus could encourage the model to repeatedly produce trivial but valid actions like "look," which over 30 turns could accumulate to 3.0 points (30% of a successful episode's 10.0 reward). The KL penalty should help keep the model anchored to the SFT distribution, but this behavior warrants monitoring during training.

I'm noticing that exp012's valid-action bonus might recreate the same problem we saw in exp010, where action_is_valid improved significantly (58.8% → 68.3%) while success actually declined (4.7% → 0%). The original issue was format_penalty driving GRPO advantages in failure-only groups, but now we're explicitly rewarding valid actions regardless of task success—which could amplify that same divergence pattern rather than fix it.

Actually, wait—I need to reconsider what "valid action" really means here. The bonus isn't just rewarding format correctness like before; it's rewarding actions that are semantically available in the current game state. That's fundamentally different from the format_penalty signal and should be a much stronger learning target. But I need to verify exactly how action validity is being checked in the environment code.

Looking at how admissible_commands works in ALFWorld, these are context-dependent valid actions—not just syntactically correct commands. So the model would only get the 0.1 bonus for actions that actually make sense in the current state. The concern is that while this is qualitatively better than the format penalty approach, it might still teach the model to produce valid actions without necessarily solving the task, since actions like "look" are almost always admissible but don't advance progress.

However, there's a mitigating factor: admissible commands include task-relevant actions like "go to dresser 1" or "pick up mug 1", and in GRPO, trajectories that take more of these task-relevant actions are more likely to accidentally complete the task and get higher rewards. So the gradient should naturally push toward solving behavior, though I should keep an eye on this pattern since it echoes the exp010 problem at a different level.

The checkpoint at step 5 is complete with all necessary model and optimizer states saved. GPUs are currently idle and exp012 hasn't started yet. I'm also noticing a potential issue with reward scale interactions—the environment reward could accumulate to around 3.0 over 30 turns, and combined with the format penalty, this might create unexpected dynamics that need closer attention.

Let me think through the actual advantage structure in GRPO. With a group of 16 trajectories where maybe one succeeds, the successful trajectory gets roughly 11-12 reward while the best failure gets around 3.0 and the worst gets 0.0. That's a clear and dominant advantage signal, which is good. But I should verify what happens in scenarios with multiple successes or different failure distributions.

When zero trajectories succeed, the model still learns from the gradient between valid and invalid actions—the 0.1 bonus per valid action creates a learning signal without drowning out the success reward when it does occur. This feels like the right balance.

I'm convinced the design is actually solid. The real question is whether learning to produce valid actions reliably translates into learning to solve tasks, but that's a stepping stone rather than a guarantee. It's definitely a step forward from the previous approach that only used format penalties.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nThis is the environment for the ALFRED dataset.\\nauthor: Qineng Wang\\ndate: 2025-03-30\\n\\nModified to match verl-agent AlfWorld implementation:\\n- Dynamic mode switching between train/val\\n- Simplified reward: score * won\\n- Admissible actions included in observation\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport os\\nimport random\\nimport textworld\\nimport textworld.gym\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom alfworld.agents.environment.alfred_tw_env import AlfredTWEnv, AlfredDemangler, AlfredInfos\\nfrom ragen.env.base import BaseLanguageBasedEnv\\nfrom ragen.env.alfworld.config import AlfredEnvConfig\\nfrom ragen.env.alfworld.utils import load_config\\n\\n_RAW_ENV_CACHE = {}\\n\\nclass AlfredTXTEnv(BaseLanguageBasedEnv):\\n\\n    # Mode mapping: RAGEN mode -> AlfWorld train_eval\\n    MODE_MAP = {\\n        'train': 'train',\\n        'val': None,  # Will use config.eval_dataset\\n        'test': None,  # Will use config.eval_dataset\\n    }\\n\\n    def __init__(self, config: AlfredEnvConfig = AlfredEnvConfig(), mode='train'):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Initialize AlfWorld environment.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            config: AlfredEnvConfig instance\\n            mode: 'train' or 'val' - determines which dataset split to use\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        super().__init__()\\n        self.config = config\\n        self.raw_env_config = load_config(self.config.config_file)\\n\\n        # Map RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval\\n        self.current_mode = mode\\n        self._alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        # Initialize raw environment\\n        self.raw_env = self._get_cached_raw_env(self._alfworld_mode)\\n        self.num_games = self.raw_env.num_games\\n        self.game_files = list(self.raw_env.game_files)\\n\\n        self.alfred_env = None\\n        self.current_game_file = None\\n        self.render_cache = None\\n        self.available_actions = None\\n        self.render_mode = self.config.render_mode\\n        assert self.render_mode == 'text'\\n\\n    def _get_alfworld_mode(self, mode: str) -> str:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Convert RAGEN mode to AlfWorld train_eval string.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if mode == 'train':\\n            return 'train'\\n        else:\\n            # Use eval_dataset from config for val/test modes\\n            return self.config.eval_dataset\\n\\n    def _get_cached_raw_env(self, alfworld_mode: str) -> AlfredTWEnv:\\n        cache_key = (os.path.abspath(self.config.config_file), alfworld_mode)\\n        raw_env = _RAW_ENV_CACHE.get(cache_key)\\n        if raw_env is None:\\n            raw_env = AlfredTWEnv(config=self.raw_env_config, train_eval=alfworld_mode)\\n            _RAW_ENV_CACHE[cache_key] = raw_env\\n        return raw_env\\n    \\n    def _reinitialize_for_mode(self, mode: str):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Reinitialize the environment when mode changes.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        new_alfworld_mode = self._get_alfworld_mode(mode)\\n\\n        if new_alfworld_mode != self._alfworld_mode:\\n            # Close existing environment\\n            if hasattr(self, 'alfred_env') and self.alfred_env is not None:\\n                try:\\n                    self.alfred_env.close()\\n                except Exception:\\n                    pass\\n\\n            # Reinitialize with new mode\\n            self._alfworld_mode = new_alfworld_mode\\n            self.current_mode = mode\\n            self.raw_env = self._get_cached_raw_env(self._alfworld_mode)\\n            self.num_games = self.raw_env.num_games\\n            self.game_files = list(self.raw_env.game_files)\\n            self.alfred_env = None\\n\\n    def reset(self, seed=None, mode=None):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Reset the environment with a specific seed.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            seed: Random seed for game selection\\n            mode: 'train', 'val', or 'test' - if different from current mode,\\n                  reinitializes the environment with the new dataset split\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            observation: Initial observation text with admissible actions\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        try:\\n            # _source line 172 · 2026-05-14T06:29:50.455Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
exp012 has crashed immediately on startup with an error:

```
TypeError: EngineArgs.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'disable_mm_preprocessor_cache'
```

This is a vLLM version compatibility issue. The code is passing `disable_mm_preprocessor_cache` to vLLM's `LLM()` constructor, but the installed version of vLLM doesn't support this parameter.

Also important from the registry notes: "D010指令：纯A2实验，隔离变量。format_penalty=0（非-0.01），不含valid-action bonus，env.py保持原始二值reward（won=10.0/otherwise 0）"


The registry notes for D010 indicate a shift from the earlier config I reviewed—format_penalty should be 0 rather than -0.01, and the valid-action bonus is removed entirely, using only the original binary reward structure. But the immediate blocker is the vLLM crash preventing the training from even starting, so I need to address that first before investigating the config discrepancies.
````

Now I have the full picture:

1. **exp012 crashed immediately** with `TypeError: EngineArgs.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'disable_mm_preprocessor_cache'`
2. **Root cause**: `agent_proxy.py:58` passes `disable_mm_preprocessor_cache=True` to vLLM's `LLM()`, but the installed vLLM version doesn't support this parameter
3. **GPUs idle**: 0 MiB, 0% on both
4. **Registry still shows RUNNING** but it's dead (EXIT_CODE=1)


5. **Config changed**: format_penalty is now 0 instead of -0.01, and there's some ambiguity around whether env.py's valid-action bonus was reverted or kept. The entry point switched to evaluation mode rather than training, which explains the "Starting evaluation process" message in the logs.

Actually, looking at this more carefully — agent_proxy.py is the training entry point for multi-turn RL in RAGEN, so the eval message is likely from a validation step before training began. The crash happened before training could actually start. I need to check whether the worker followed D010's instructions on env.py.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Critical finding! exp012 has **crashed** with an error:

```
TypeError: EngineArgs.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'disable_mm_preprocessor_cache'
```

This is a vLLM version incompatibility error. The code is passing `disable_mm_preprocessor_cache` to vLLM's `LLM()` class, but the installed vLLM version doesn't support this argument.

Also interesting:
- The checkpoint directory already has `global_step_6` - this suggests there was a previous successful run (probably from an earlier attempt or the process ran for a bit before crashing on a second attempt)


- Looking at the timestamps, the log was last modified at 14:28 while the global_step_6 directory was created at 14:25, which means exp012 started, ran briefly, and crashed during initialization before any training actually occurred.
- The registry shows exp012 was created at 06:24, but the checkpoint at 14:25 suggests this was from a prior successful run that completed at least one step, and what we're seeing now is a failed restart attempt where the initialization is failing.
- The log path discrepancy is interesting—the registry lists a path that doesn't exist, while the actual log shows the crash happened during VllmWrapperWg initialization before training could start.
- The GPU memory reading of 0 MiB is suspicious and might indicate the process never actually allocated resources, which could explain why initialization failed.
- The registry still shows RUNNING status even though the process is clearly dead, so I need to verify if the process is actually alive or if the status is stale.
````

_source line 205 · 2026-05-14T06:30:33.958Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the full picture:

1. **exp012 crashed on startup** — `agent_proxy.py:58` passes `disable_mm_preprocessor_cache=True` to vLLM `LLM()`, but the installed vLLM version doesn't support this parameter.

2. **env.py**: The `grep -A5 "# D007"` returned exit code 1 (no match), which means either:
   - The D007 comment was removed (env.py was reverted to original binary reward)
   - Or the comment format is different

Let me check env.py step function to see if valid-action bonus was reverted per D010.


The registry shows RUNNING but the process exited with code 1, so it's stale. The config has format_penalty set to 0 (updated from -0.01 per D010). The real issue is that the training command used `python -m ragen.llm_agent.agent_proxy` instead of `python train.py` — that's the eval path using vLLM directly, not the verl training pipeline. The monitor worker should've caught this command mismatch.

Looking at experiment 012's registry, it's using the same agent_proxy entry point that previous experiments used train.py for. The logs confirm it's running in eval mode, not training. If the worker intended to start GRPO training, using agent_proxy.py is the wrong entry point entirely.

But I should verify whether agent_proxy.py might serve dual purposes in the current RAGEN version, or if the worker is running eval first to establish baseline metrics before training begins. Let me check what the actual training entry point should be.
```

</details>

_source line 216 · 2026-05-14T06:30:50.068Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
So:
- `/root/autodl-tmp/exp012_grpo.log` doesn't exist
- `/root/runs/cost-verify-agent-rl/exp012.log` exists and shows the crash
- `global_step_6` checkpoint has only an `actor` directory (no optimizer state), created at 14:25 local time

The crash error: `TypeError: EngineArgs.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'disable_mm_preprocessor_cache'`

This is a vLLM API incompatibility. The code is using a newer vLLM API parameter that the installed version doesn't support.
 
But exp010 and exp011 succeeded with the same codebase, so something's different here. The key difference is the entry point—exp010 ran through `train.py` while exp012 uses `ragen.llm_agent.agent_proxy`, which likely has its own vLLM initialization that's passing unsupported parameters. The fact that a checkpoint exists at step 6 means training did start successfully before hitting this incompatibility issue. The checkpoint at step 6 was saved with just the actor weights but no optimizer state, which suggests the training did complete those steps but the evaluation phase crashed before it could properly save everything. The critical issue is that exp012 shows as RUNNING in the registry but the process has actually crashed with 0% GPU usage. I also need to verify that env.py was correctly reverted to use the binary reward without the valid-action bonus, which aligns with the pure A2 setup.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have complete clarity:

1. **env.py has been REVERTED** to original binary reward: `reward = self.config.score * float(won)` (line 195). The valid-action bonus from my earlier review was removed. D010 instructed "env.py保持原始二值reward" and that's been done.

2. **exp012 crashed** with vLLM compatibility error: `disable_mm_preprocessor_cache` not supported. The entry point `python -m ragen.llm_agent.agent_proxy` is wrong — previous successful experiments used `python train.py`. 

The issue is that agent_proxy.py invokes vLLM directly, whereas train.py routes through the verl training pipeline, so this is fundamentally the wrong execution path.

3. **Registry shows RUNNING but exp012 is dead** — needs status update.

4. **GPUs are idle** — confirmed 0 MiB, 0%.

The core problem is clear: exp012 failed on startup due to both an incompatible vLLM parameter and using the wrong entry point entirely.

The monitor worker completed its check on exp012's early steps and confirmed the crash happened immediately—training never initialized. Meanwhile, D010 is properly isolated with binary rewards and no format penalty, exactly as specified.

Now I'm seeing the registry shows exp012 as RUNNING even though the process exited with code 1, and both GPUs are sitting idle after exp011 was killed and exp012 crashed. This is a significant resource waste that needs addressing.
```

</details>

Now I see the full picture:

1. **exp012 log shows only the crash log** — there's no evidence of successful training in this log file. The entry point used is `python -m ragen.llm_agent.agent_proxy` which is the eval/inference entry point, not `python train.py`.

2. **But global_step_6 checkpoint exists** with complete model + optimizer state (both ranks). This means either:
   - exp012 ran with `train.py` first (successful training for 1 step), then the eval was attempted separately with `agent_proxy` and crashed
   - Or the worker ran training first, then tried to verify with eval and it failed
   
3. **The registry command is actually running the eval/inference tool**, not the trainer. Looking at the full reproduce command, it's setting up the environment and calling `agent_proxy` with the config, which explains why the log only shows the crash from that inference attempt rather than training output. The checkpoint at `global_step_6` was likely created by a separate training process rather than this eval attempt, since the registry shows the process crashed immediately during initialization. Looking at the timestamps, the checkpoint was created about a minute after the registry entry, which aligns with training starting right away. Let me search for other logs and tmux sessions to confirm whether training actually ran.

Actually, I'm overcomplicating this. The core issue is clear: the exp012 process has crashed and isn't running, the GPUs are idle, and the crash was caused by a vLLM version incompatibility with the `disable_mm_preprocessor_cache` parameter. There's a partial checkpoint at `global_step_6` showing at least one step completed from the baseline, but I need to determine if that came from this process or another one.

Looking at the configuration, `env.py` has been correctly reverted to use binary rewards as required by D010, and the config is properly set with `format_penalty=0` and `group_size=16`. The registry still shows the experiment as RUNNING even though the actual process is dead — that's a stale status that needs to be cleared.
